Proposition 8.15
.

The functors
\Sigma^{\infty}
and
\Omega^{\infty}
take
\mathbb{J}_{0}
-algebras to
\mathbb{J}_{0}
-algebras and the (topological) adjunction

G\scr{S}(\Sigma^{\infty}X,E)\cong G\scr{T}(X,\Omega^{\infty}E)

induces a (topological) adjunction

\mathbb{J}_{0}[G\scr{S}](\Sigma^{\infty}Z,R)\cong\mathbb{J}_{0}[G\scr{T}](Z,\Omega^{\infty}R).

Thus the monad
Q=\Omega^{\infty}\Sigma^{\infty}
on
G\scr{T}
restricts to a monad
Q
on

\mathbb{J}_{0}[G\scr{T}]
and, when restricted to
\mathbb{J}_{0}
-
G
-spectra, the
functor
\Omega^{\infty}
takes values in
\mathbb{J}_{0}
-
G
-spaces.

Proof.

This is a formal consequence of the fact that the
isomorphism (
8.11
) is monadic, as is explained in
general categorical terms in
[
May09b
, Appendix A]
.
If
(Z,\xi)
is a
\mathbb{J}_{0}
-
G
-space,
then
\Sigma^{\infty}Z
is a
\mathbb{J}_{0}
-
G
-spectrum with structure map

\textstyle{\mathbb{J}_{0}\Sigma^{\infty}Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\Sigma^{\infty}\mathbb{J}_{0}Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma^{\infty}\xi}
\textstyle{\Sigma^{\infty}Z.}

The isomorphism (
8.11
) and the
adjunction give a natural composite
\delta
:

\textstyle{\mathbb{J}_{0}\Omega^{\infty}E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\textstyle{\Omega^{\infty}\Sigma^{\infty}\mathbb{J}_{0}\Omega^{\infty}E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\Omega^{\infty}\mathbb{J}_{0}\Sigma^{\infty}\Omega^{\infty}E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Omega^{\infty}\mathbb{J}_{0}\varepsilon}
\textstyle{\Omega^{\infty}\mathbb{J}_{0}E.}

If
(R,\xi)
is a
\mathbb{J}_{0}
-
G
-spectrum, then

\Omega^{\infty}R
is a
\mathbb{J}_{0}
-
G
-space with structure map

\textstyle{\mathbb{J}_{0}\Omega^{\infty}R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta}
\textstyle{\Omega^{\infty}\mathbb{J}_{0}R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Omega^{\infty}\xi}
\textstyle{\Omega^{\infty}R.}

Diagram chases show that the unit
\eta
and counit
\varepsilon
of the
adjunction are maps of
\mathbb{J}_{0}
-algebras when
Z
and
R
are
\mathbb{J}_{0}
-algebras, and the restricted adjunction follows.
∎

Taking
X=Y_{+}
, with the basepoint thought of as
0
and using the notation of
(8.12)
, this has the following consequence.

Corollary 8.16
.

The adjunction of Proposition
8.15
induces an adjunction

\mathbb{J}_{0}[G\scr{S}](\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}Y,R)\cong\mathbb{J}[G\scr{U}](Y,\Omega^{\infty}R)

between the category of
\mathbb{J}_{0}
-
G
-spectra

(alias
E_{\infty}
-ring
G
-spectra) and the category of
\mathbb{J}
-
G
-spaces
Y
,
where
\mathbb{J}
-
G
-spaces are unbased
\mathbb{J}
-spaces, that is
\mathbb{J}
-spaces without
0
.

Proof.

Recall that we have the obvious adjunction

G\scr{T}(Y_{+},X)\cong G\scr{U}(Y,iX)

for based
G
-spaces
X
and unbased
G
-spaces
Y
, where
i\colon\scr{T}\longrightarrow\scr{U}
is given by forgetting basepoints. It induces an adjunction

\mathbb{J}_{0}[G\scr{T}](Y_{+},Z)\cong\mathbb{J}[G\scr{U}](Y,Z)

for
\mathbb{J}
-
G
-spaces
Y
and
\mathbb{J}_{0}
-
G
-spaces
Z
regarded on the right as
\mathbb{J}
-spaces by forgetting the basepoint
0
. Taking
Z=\Omega^{\infty}R
, the result follows by composing with the adjunction of Proposition
8.15
.
∎

Fixing
(\scr{C},\scr{J})
, we take
(\scr{C},\scr{J})
-
G
-spaces as our

ring
G

\mathbb{J}_{0}
-

Corollary 8.17
.

The
0^{th}

G
-spaces of
E_{\infty}
ring
G
-spectra are
naturally
E_{\infty}
ring
G
-spaces.

The following result makes clear that
Section 1.1
restricts multiplicatively to another instance of
Section 1.1
.

Corollary 8.18
.

The natural action of the monad
\mathbb{C}
in
G\scr{T}
on
\Omega^{\infty}E
for
G
-spectra
E
restricts on
\mathbb{J}_{0}
-
G
-spectra
R
to a natural action of the monad
\mathbb{C}
in
\mathbb{J}_{0}[G\scr{T}]
.

Proof.

Recall that
\mathbb{C}
maps to
\mathbb{K}
and
\mathbb{K}
acts naturally on
\Omega^{\infty}E
. The action is induced as in (i) of
Theorem 1.16
from the unit and counit of the monad
\Omega^{\infty}\Sigma^{\infty}
, and those are maps of
\mathbb{J}_{0}
-algebras when their input is given by
\mathbb{J}_{0}
-algebras.
∎

8.6.
Assumptions B through F and the ring completion theorem

Forgetting from
(6.57)
down to the diagram without the
\mathbb{J}_{0}
, we define the weak equivalences of
Section 2.1
to be created in the ground categories of
G
-spaces and
G
-spectra. Similarly,
Section 2.1
is satisfied, with the notion of
\Omega^{\infty}
-connective
G
-spectrum inherited from
G\scr{S}
.
We say that an
E_{\infty}
ring
G
-space is ringlike if it is grouplike as a
G
-space, and we say that a map of
E_{\infty}
ring
G
-spaces is a ring completion if its underlying map of
G
-spaces is a group completion. Checking that the group completion functor
(\mathbb{G},g)
preserves
E_{\infty}
ring
G
-spaces, we have
Section 2.2
by specialization of its additive version to
(\mathbb{C},\mathbb{J}_{0})
-algebras.

The approximation theorem,
Section 2.3
, concerns the underlying additive structure and is therefore immediate from its additive version. The essential formal point is that if
X
is a
\mathbb{J}_{0}
-algebra, then
\alpha\colon\mathbb{C}X\longrightarrow\Omega^{\infty}\Sigma^{\infty}X=QX
is a map of
\mathbb{J}_{0}
-
G
-algebras.
Finally, the formal parts of
Section 2.4
are verified as in
Section 2.4
. Its homotopical part (vii) follows from its analog for
G
-spectra.
Here we need that, for a simplicial
\mathbb{J}_{0}
-algebra
\scr{K}_{*}
,
\gamma\colon|\Omega^{\infty}\scr{K}|\longrightarrow\Omega^{\infty}|\scr{K}|
is a map of
\mathbb{J}_{0}
-
G
-algebras, and that is verified by inspection of linear isometries in the passage to colimits used in the additive verification.

Theorem 8.19
.

For
E_{\infty}
ring
G
-spaces
Y
, there is a natural ring completion

Y\longrightarrow\Omega^{\infty}_{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{E}Y\cong\Omega^{\infty}_{\mathbb{C}}\Sigma^{\infty}_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{Y},

and any connective
E_{\infty}
ring
G
-spectrum
E
is equivalent to
\Sigma^{\infty}_{\mathbb{C}}\Omega^{\infty}_{\mathbb{C}}E
.

9.
Composite adjunctions and the multiplicative theory

9.1.
Multiplicative structure in the composite adjunction context

Just as we applied our original general context of
Section 1.1
multiplicatively by incorporating multiplicative structure into the given ground categories
\scr{T}
and
\scr{S}
, we can apply our composite adjunction context of
Section 5.1
by additionally incorporating multiplicative structure into the given ground category
\scr{V}
. Thus suppose that, in addition to
Section 5.1
, we are given a monad pair
(\mathbb{C},\mathbb{J}_{0})
on
\scr{T}
. Remembering that
\varepsilon\colon\mathbb{L}\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow\mathrm{id}
is an isomorphism, we can transfer
(\mathbb{C},\mathbb{J}_{0})
to
\scr{V}
via the adjunction
(\mathbb{L},\mathbb{R})
between
\scr{V}
and
\scr{T}
.

Proposition 9.1
.

Let
\mathbb{D}=\mathbb{R}\mathbb{C}\mathbb{L}
and
\mathbb{K}_{0}=\mathbb{R}\mathbb{J}_{0}\mathbb{L}
. Then
\mathbb{D}
and
\mathbb{K}_{0}
are
monads on
\scr{V}
, and the action of
\mathbb{J}_{0}
on
\mathbb{C}
induces an action of

\mathbb{K}_{0}
on
\mathbb{D}
, so that
(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{K}_{0})
is a monad pair in
\scr{V}
. The adjunction
(\mathbb{L},\mathbb{R})
restricts to an adjunction from
\mathbb{K}_{0}[\scr{V}]
to
\mathbb{J}_{0}[\scr{T}]
. Moreover, with

\omega=\mathbb{R}\mathbb{C}\varepsilon\colon\mathbb{D}\mathbb{R}=\mathbb{R}\mathbb{C}\mathbb{L}\mathbb{R}\cong\mathbb{R}\mathbb{C},

the diagrams of
Section 5.1
are diagrams in
\mathbb{J}_{0}[G\scr{T}]
and, with

\omega_{\otimes}=\mathbb{R}\mathbb{J}_{0}\varepsilon\colon\mathbb{K}_{0}\mathbb{R}=\mathbb{R}\mathbb{J}_{0}\mathbb{L}\mathbb{R}\cong\mathbb{R}\mathbb{J}_{0},

the analogous multiplicative diagrams (displayed in
Section 9.1
below) also commute.

Proof.

The first claim, that
\mathbb{D}
and
\mathbb{K}_{0}
are monads, follows from
Proposition 5.9
.
To prove the second claim, we use the descriptions in

Theorem 8.2
(ii). We have
\mu_{\mathbb{C},\mathbb{J}_{0}}:\mathbb{C}\mathbb{J}_{0}\mathbb{C}\mathbb{J}_{0}\to\mathbb{C}\mathbb{J}_{0}
for the action of
\mathbb{J}_{0}
on
\mathbb{C}
. It is easy to verify formally that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\mathbb{D},\mathbb{K}_{0}}:\mathbb{D}\mathbb{K}_{0}\mathbb{D}\mathbb{K}_{0}\cong\mathbb{R}\mathbb{C}\mathbb{J}_{0}\mathbb{C}\mathbb{J}_{0}\mathbb{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathbb{C}\mathbb{J}_{0}\mathbb{L}\cong\mathbb{D}\mathbb{K}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mu_{\mathbb{C},\mathbb{J}_{0}}\mathbb{L}}

satisfies
Theorem 8.2
(ii), establishing an action of
\mathbb{K}_{0}
on
\mathbb{D}
. For the restricted adjunction, a given action of
\mathbb{J}_{0}
on
Y
induces an action of
\mathbb{K}_{0}
on
\mathbb{R}\mathbb{Y}
and a given action of
\mathbb{K}_{0}
on
X
induces an action of
\mathbb{J}_{0}
on
\mathbb{L}X
since
\mathbb{K}_{0}=\mathbb{R}\mathbb{J}_{0}\mathbb{L}
and
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{R}=\operatorname{Id}
. Easy diagram chases give the rest.
∎

This gives an example of pairs
(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{K}_{0})
for which the following multiplicative elaboration of
Section 5.1
holds.

Assumption A
mcom
.
We assume
Section 5.1
and assume further that

(i)

we have monad pairs
(\mathbb{C},\mathbb{J}_{0})
in

and

\mathbb{K}_{0}[\scr{V}]

.

(ii)

the isomorphism

and the diagrams in

are diagrams of functors

.

(iii)

we have an isomorphism

such that the following analogous diagrams commute.

Section 9.1
, the diagram

embeds into the following diagram, which is a multiplicative version of the general composite adjunction diagram of

As in
Corollary 5.18
, our assumptions imply that the arrows
\mathbb{R}
are obtained by specialization of their underlying non-multiplicative arrows
\mathbb{R}
. We have assumed that we have the solid arrow adjunction
(\mathbb{L},\mathbb{R})
at the top. As in the original additive composite adjunction context, the dotted arrow
\mathbb{L}
may or may not exist, but the functor
(\Sigma\mathbb{L})_{\mathbb{D}}
always exists nonetheless, by application of

.

From here, just as in
Section 8.6
in the context of
Section 1.1
, the answer to the multiplicative analog of Question
5.2
is almost exactly the same as is discussed in the underlying additive theory in
Section 5
. In fact, our Assumptions are essentially all satisfied when they are satisfied by the underlying additive structures. Weak equivalences and connectivity (Assumptions
5.1
and
2.1
) are defined by reference to the underlying categories (
\scr{T}
,
\scr{S}
,
\scr{V}
).

Group completion
Section 2.2
(alias ring completion here) and the approximation theorem (
Section 2.3
) are seen in the underlying additive theory.
Section 5.4
requires that realization in the underlying categories carries over to give realization in the new ground categories (
\mathbb{J}_{0}[\scr{T}]
,
\mathbb{J}_{0}[\scr{S}]
,
\mathbb{K}_{0}[\scr{V}]
), and then most of its required properties (i)-(vii) fall out from their being satisfied in the underlying additive context. Only (vi), that
\gamma
preserves the underlying multiplicative structure, requires any real verification beyond the additive verifications. When these Assumptions hold, Theorems
5.31
and
5.32
hold with
\scr{V}
replaced by

\mathbb{K}_{0}[\scr{V}]
and
\scr{S}
replaced by
\mathbb{J}_{0}[\scr{S}]
.

Theorem 9.3
.

There is a functor
\mathrm{Bar}\colon\mathbb{D}\big[\mathbb{K}_{0}[\scr{V}]\big]\longrightarrow\mathbb{D}\big[\mathbb{K}_{0}[\scr{V}]\big]
, written
Y\mapsto\overline{Y}
, and a natural equivalence
\zeta\colon\overline{Y}\longrightarrow Y
given by a map of
(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{K}_{0})
-algebras. If
Y
is special, the unit
\eta_{\mathbb{D}}\colon\overline{Y}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{R}\Omega)_{\mathbb{D}}(\Sigma\mathbb{L})_{\mathbb{D}}\overline{Y}
is a ring completion and is therefore an equivalence if
Y
is ringlike.

Theorem 9.4
.

\big((\Sigma\mathbb{L})_{\mathbb{D}},(\mathbb{R}\Omega)_{\mathbb{D}}\big)
induces an adjoint equivalence from the homotopy category of special ringlike
(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{K}_{0})
-algebras in
\scr{V}
to the homotopy category of
\Omega
-connective objects of
\mathbb{J}_{0}[\scr{S}]
.

9.2.
Monad pairs in the categories of operators context

We defined categories of operators and their associated monads in
6.2
and
6.4
, working equivariantly as in
[
MMO
]
. The nonequivariant multiplicative elaboration to pairs of categories of operators and their associated monad pairs is developed in

[
May09a
]
, but the equivariant elaboration is not in the literature. We briefly develop it here, largely following the nonequivariant treatment in
[
May09a
]
, especially part of its Section 10. That part is essentially in our present composite of adjunctions context, but it was written before the context was understood. It appeared there as a component of a more elaborate picture aimed at a machine constructing
E_{\infty}
ring spectra from bipermutative categories. Since we shall treat that differently and equivariantly
in
[
May24
]
, we shall be brief here.

We saw earlier that an operad pair
(\scr{C},\scr{J})
of
G
-spaces gives rise to a monad pair
(\mathbb{C},\mathbb{J})
. The pair
(\scr{C},\scr{J})
also gives rise to a pair of categories of operators over
\scr{F}
and a
G
-operad pair
(\scr{C}_{G},\scr{J}_{G})
gives rise to a pair of categories of operators over
\scr{F}_{G}
. These pairs of categories of operators give rise to canonical pairs of monads
(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{K}_{0})
and
(\mathbb{D}_{G},{\mathbb{K}_{G}}_{0})
as constructed in
Section 6.4
. However, as pointed out nonequivariantly in
[
May09a
, p. 315]
, it is not true that these pairs of monads are monad pairs in Beck’s sense prescribed in
Section 8
:
\mathbb{K}_{0}
does not act on
\mathbb{D}
and
{\mathbb{K}_{G}}_{0}
does not act on
{\mathbb{D}_{G}}_{0}
. Moreover, as is easily seen from the definitions, again already nonequivariantly, our left adjoint
\mathbb{L}
does not restrict to a functor
\mathbb{K}_{0}[\Pi[\scr{T}]]\longrightarrow\mathbb{J}_{0}[\scr{T}]
.

A way around this was explained in some detail in
[
May09a
]
, and we shall just summarize the main points.
In
[
May09a
]
, all basepoint information was ignored, correcting
[
May82
]
in which both additive and multiplicative basepoint identifications were made. The halfway house of using the additive basepoint
0
and not the multiplicative basepoint
1
seems preferable. With this change and corresponding small modifications,
32
32

32

including considerable change of notation.

[
May09a
, Section 10]
constructs a multiplicative monad
\mathbb{K}^{\prime}_{0}
, different from the canonical monad
\mathbb{K}_{0}
, that does act on the canonical monad
\mathbb{D}
.

The construction of
\mathbb{K}^{\prime}_{0}
is formal. There is a wreath product construction between categories of operators
[
May09a
, Definition 5.1]
, and there is a wreath product
\scr{K}_{0}\int\Pi
with associated monad
\overline{\mathbb{K}}_{0}
in
(\Pi\int\Pi)
-
G
-spaces
[
May09a
, Definition 9.1]
. Its algebras are the
(\scr{K}_{0}\int\Pi)
-algebras. There is an adjoint pair
(\mathbb{L}^{\prime},\mathbb{R}^{\prime})
from
(\Pi\int\Pi)
-
G
-spaces to

\Pi
-
G
-spaces. The monad
\mathbb{K}_{0}^{\prime}
is the conjugate
\mathbb{L}^{\prime}\overline{\mathbb{K}}_{0}\mathbb{R}^{\prime}

[
May09a
, Definition 9.4]
. It admits a concrete description that starts from an isomorphism
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{K}^{\prime}_{0}\cong\mathbb{J}_{0}\mathbb{L}

[
May09a
, Lemma 10.4]
and builds from there, but that is not detailed in
[
May09a
]
. Since we do not find it illuminating, we shall also not detail it here. There is an analogous construction starting with monads in

\Pi_{G}[\underline{G\scr{T}}]
and
(\Pi_{G}\int\Pi_{G})[\underline{G\scr{T}}]
.

The upshot is that we obtain the following multiplicative version of the diagram
(6.57)
. Both the upper and lower parts are examples of the multiplicative composite adjunction context of
(9.2)
.
We have just changed ground categories to include the multiplicative structure.

(9.5)

\textstyle{\mathbb{K}^{\prime}_{0}\big[\Pi[\underline{G\scr{T}}]\big]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{L}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{F}_{\mathbb{D}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{J}_{0}[G\scr{T}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{R}}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma^{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{F}_{\mathbb{C}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{J}_{0}[G\scr{S}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Omega^{\infty}}

\scriptstyle{(\mathbb{R}\Omega^{\infty})_{\mathbb{D}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathbb{C}\big[\mathbb{J}_{0}[G\scr{T}]\big]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

The essential point is that, with
\scr{V}=\Pi[\underline{G\scr{T}}]
or
\scr{V}=\Pi_{G}[\underline{G\scr{T}}]
, both diagrams in
(9.5)
are special cases of the diagram
(9.2)
. The assumptions are all verified with no more trouble than in the nonequivariant case. We state the specializations of Theorems
5.31
and
5.32
to categories of operators over
\scr{F}
, and we have a compatible analog for categories of operators over
\scr{F}_{G}
.

Theorem 9.6
.

There is a functor
\mathrm{Bar}\colon\mathbb{D}\big[\mathbb{K}^{\prime}_{0}[\Pi[G\scr{T}]]\big]\longrightarrow\mathbb{D}\big[\mathbb{K}^{\prime}_{0}[\Pi[G\scr{T}]]\big]
, written
Y\mapsto\overline{Y}
, and a natural equivalence
\zeta\colon\overline{Y}\longrightarrow Y
given by a map of
(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{K}^{\prime}_{0})
-algebras. If
Y
is special, the unit
\eta_{\mathbb{D}}\colon\overline{Y}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{R}\Omega^{\infty})_{\mathbb{D}}(\Sigma^{\infty}\mathbb{L})_{\mathbb{D}}\overline{Y}
is a ring completion and is therefore an equivalence if
Y
is ringlike.

Theorem 9.7
.

\big((\Sigma^{\infty}\mathbb{L})_{\mathbb{D}},(\mathbb{R}\Omega^{\infty})_{\mathbb{D}}\big)
induces an adjoint equivalence from the homotopy category of special ringlike
(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{K}^{\prime}_{0})
-algebras to the homotopy category of
\Omega^{\infty}
-connective
G
-spectra in
\mathbb{J}_{0}[G\scr{S}]
.

The main use of categories of operators is to allow comparison with Segalic infinite loop space theory and to apply infinite loop space theory to go from symmetric bimonoidal categories to
E_{\infty}
ring spectra. For the former, multiplicative elaboration of
[
MMO
]
seems entirely doable, but modulo the limitations described in
Remark 6.55
. We shall not follow that up here. For the latter, a good definition of symmetric bimonoidal
G
-categories has long eluded us. That will be given in
[
May24
]
, together with a shortcut that circumvents the use of categories of operators in the passage from such categories to
E_{\infty}
ring
G
-spectra. That second use was the focus of much of
[
May82
,
May09a
]
, and it is still needed for purposes of comparison, as will be explained in
[
May24
]
.

9.3.
Monad pairs in the orbital presheaves context

Here we take
\scr{V}=G\scr{O}^{op}[\scr{T}]
and assume that we have monad pairs there that satisfy the axioms, as in
Section 9.1
.
33
33

33

The construction and application of such monad pairs is work in progress.
Starting from operads
\scr{C}
, the key additive monads are constructed in
[
KMZ24
]
, where they are called
\mathbb{C}^{pre}
as in
Remark 7.3
. The model categorical arguments of
Section 7.2
go through to give model structures on the categories
\mathbb{J}_{0}[G\scr{T}]
and
\mathbb{D}\big[\mathbb{Q}_{0}[G\scr{O}^{op}[\scr{T}]]\big]
such that the following enhancement of
Proposition 7.5
holds.

Proposition 9.8
.

The Quillen equivalence
(\mathbb{L},\mathbb{R})
between the categories
\mathbb{C}[G\scr{T}]
and
\mathbb{D}\big[G\mathscr{O}^{op}[\scr{T}]_{ss}\big]
restricts to an isomorphism of categories between the full subcategories of cofibrant
(\mathbb{C},\mathbb{J}_{0})
-algebras and cofibrant
(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{K}_{0})
-algebras. Therefore
(\mathbb{L},\mathbb{R})
restricts to an equivalence between
\mathbb{C}\big[\mathbb{J}_{0}[G\scr{T}]\big]
and
\mathbb{D}\big[\mathbb{K}_{0}[G\mathscr{O}^{op}[\scr{T}]_{ss}]\big]
.

This leads us to a multiplicatively enriched version of the diagram
(7.6)
. In contrast with the categories of operators context, we are only using canonical monads as constructed in
Section 6.4
. The top part does not fit into
Section 5.1
, but it is where interesting examples would live. After application of

\Gamma
, we land in a context where
Section 5.1
does hold, by
Proposition 9.1
and
Corollary 5.18
applied to both the additive and multiplicative monads.

In this context, we have the following versions of Theorems
5.31
and
5.32
.

Theorem 9.10
.

There is a functor
\mathrm{Bar}\colon\mathbb{D}\big[\mathbb{K}_{0}\big[G\scr{O}^{op}[\scr{T}]\big]\longrightarrow\mathbb{D}\big[\mathbb{K}_{0}\big[G\scr{O}^{op}[\scr{T}]\big]
, written
Y\mapsto\overline{Y}
, and a natural equivalence
\zeta\colon\overline{Y}\longrightarrow Y
given by a map of
(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{K}_{0})
-algebras. If
Y
and therefore
\bar{Y}
are strictly special, as can be arranged by applying
\Gamma
, the unit
\eta_{\mathbb{D}}\colon\overline{Y}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{R}\Omega^{\infty})_{\mathbb{D}}(\Sigma^{\infty}\mathbb{L})_{\mathbb{D}}\overline{Y}
is a ring completion and is therefore an equivalence if
Y
is ringlike.

Theorem 9.11
.

After application of
\Gamma
,
\big((\Sigma^{\infty}\mathbb{L})_{\mathbb{D}},(\mathbb{R}\Omega^{\infty})_{\mathbb{D}}\big)
induces an adjoint equivalence from the homotopy category of strictly special ringlike
(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{K}_{0})
-algebras to the homotopy category of
\Omega^{\infty}
-connective
G
-spectra in
\mathbb{J}_{0}[G\scr{S}]
.

9.4.
Composites of composite adjunctions

We close by simply observing that our context is broad enough to likely have many other applications. For an evident example, it is reasonable to compose several adjunctions in the composite adjunction context. For example, the fixed point adjunction can be composed with the categories of operators adjunction, giving a categorical version of orbital presheaves. We do not have present applications of that in mind, but it does suggest how a monadically generalized Segal machine might be constructed on categorical orbital presheaves. A direction worthy of thought is application of the ideas here to motivic stable homotopy theory.
